| 2026-09-30 | MU | bull won — Operating/guidance test, qualified; stock reaction and Q&A not assessed. Micron / Sanjay Mehrotra and Mark Murphy, September 30 prepared remarks: FQ4 revenue $54.2bn, non-GAAP GM 87.0%, EPS $33.42; FQ1 guide $61.5bn +/- $1.5bn, GM ~86.25%, EPS $38.15 +/- $1.00. Against the frozen [Capstone September 29 preview](../../outputs/mu-preview-2026-09-29/MU_FQ4FY26_Preview_2026-09-29.md), November revenue clears BofA's September 26 investor poll ($60bn), EPS reaches the lower end of Barclays/Rand's September 28 $38-39 bar, but GM misses BBG September 29 (86.73%), the BofA poll (87.3%) and UBS/Arcuri September 23 (88.6%). Management expects Q1 to be the FY27 GM floor, tighter memory conditions in CY27-28, and sequential revenue growth each FY27 quarter. Capex ~$25bn in 1H with a higher 2H implies more than roughly $50bn for FY27 (inference, not a full-year point guide), versus BBG $46.28bn / UBS ~$51bn. FQ4 FCF $33.2bn excludes $12.3bn financing-classified customer deposits; December 9 capital-return timing reiterated without a new amount. SCAs 26 / RPO ~$150bn strengthen visibility, but annual floor economics, HBM de-spec impact and a dated buyback quantum remain unresolved. Source: [user-supplied company prepared remarks](C:/Users/Felipe.monteiro/Downloads/Q4-FY26-Prepared-Remarks.pdf), pp. 3-10; assessment limited to that document. Prior forecast vintages preserved. |

Management expects Q1 to be the FY27 GM floor, tighter memory conditions in CY27-28, and sequential revenue growth each FY27 quarter. Capex ~$25bn in 1H with a higher 2H implies more than roughly $50bn for FY27 (inference, not a full-year point guide), versus BBG $46.28bn / UBS ~$51bn. FQ4 FCF $33.2bn excludes $12.3bn financing-classified customer deposits; December 9 capital-return timing reiterated without a new amount. SCAs 26 / RPO ~$150bn strengthen visibility, but annual floor economics, HBM de-spec impact and a dated buyback quantum remain unresolved. Source: [user-supplied company prepared remarks](C:/Users/Felipe.monteiro/Downloads/Q4-FY26-Prepared-Remarks.pdf), pp. 3-10; assessment limited to that document. Prior forecast vintages preserved. |

| 2026-09-16 | ON | bear won — **The Financial Analyst Day RESOLVED this page's single stated test and the stock still fell 9%.** The test was UBS's own preview line: *"it would be SURPRISING TO US if ON were to MAINTAIN its previous 53% GM target given the bridge required from the CURRENT ~39% LEVEL."* **ON MAINTAINED the 53%, itemised the bridge (+650bps utilisation / +225bps Fab Right / +400bps new products & mix), RAISED the 2026-30 revenue CAGR to +12-14% from +10-12% and RAISED the FCF margin to 30-35% from 25-30%.** ⚠️ **Scored BEAR WON anyway, and deliberately, because of what paid for it: the OPERATING-MARGIN target was CUT to ~38% from 40% to fund higher R&D — a company spending the gross-margin bridge rather than dropping it to the bottom line, which is the opposite of the operating-leverage case that has carried the bull argument since the Q2 print. And the ~$47bn 2030 AI-DC TAM was reiterated while its CAGR was re-cut from ON's OWN 72% to a Street-derived 40%, which (derived: same endpoint, two CAGRs) silently re-bases the 2026 starting TAM from ~$5.4bn to ~$12.2bn.** Net-new positives that did land: a **MAJOR vCORE SOCKET secured with revenue from 2026-end** (first named rack-level win on the page), **EPP** (wafer-as-package), **vGaN sampling at 20% better efficiency / 20% smaller**, **100% of FCF returned** at low capital intensity (GS). ⚠️ **The houses disagree about the reaction, not about any number: GS calls −9% "overdone" against 2030 targets "slightly ahead of the Street (VA)"; UBS says the model merely matched its own preview while the demand backdrop had improved since that model was set. This page's own 09-16 datapoint favours GS — ON entered the day as the LEAST-preferred analog long in the Barclays survey (3%, down from 10% in mid-June).** ➤ Next dated tests: **800VDC hardware (Barclays: "still 12-18mo out")**, the **vCore socket revenue starting 2026-end**, and whether **auto normalises in Q3/Q4 FY26** — the falsifier this page set in August and which the Analyst Day did not touch. |

| 2026-09-16 | CIEN | bull won — **The Investor Forum (Ottawa) DELIVERED an unforced FY29 framework two weeks after the F3Q26 print, and the sell-side reaction the next day moved two of the three price targets on the page hard upward.** Framework: **~30% FY26-FY29 revenue CAGR against a ~24% market, ~$14bn of FY29 revenue, ~50% gross margin, ~32-35% operating margin.** 🔴 **The reason it scores bull: the gap to consensus is now a printed number on the revenue line for the first time — JPM marks FY29 at "$14bn v. $14.1bn JPMe and $12.9bn CONSENSUS" (~8.5% above the Street), with ~50% GM v. 47.5% JPMe. Combined with the 2029 margin gap already on the page (company ~50% GM / 32-35% OM vs consensus 3FY 46.0% / 28.1%), the divergence is complete across revenue, gross margin AND operating margin.** PTs: **Barclays $475→$548 (+15%), MS $425→$450, JPM $605 unchanged**; MS raised FY28 EPS to $18.44 from $16.99 and sees ~$20 CY28 / ~$25 FY29 EPS at "~17x CY28 implied". ⚠️ **THE TWO CAVEATS THAT KEEP THIS FROM BEING A CLEAN WIN, both from the bulls themselves: (1) JPM says the FY29 EPS ($25-27) is "ahead of consensus but IN LINE with JPMe and (in Joe's estimation) BUYSIDE", held back by higher-than-expected OPEX — so the EPS edge to the marginal holder may already be closed even while the revenue/GM gaps are not. (2) MS: of the three overhangs (component supply, component price increases, and the read that absent GM leverage means competition), vertical integration addresses only the third, and "for much of the NEXT YEAR… 1 and 2 unlikely to FULLY LIFT."** ⚠️ **First bear voice logged the same day: Jefferies (covering Nokia) uses Ciena's own targets as Nokia's bull case — Nokia optical +~31% in 2027/28, hyperscaler backlog EUR5bn→EUR6bn from ZERO in Q1 2025, 800G coherent pluggable wins at two hyperscalers, and 20x front-end / 10x back-end capacity additions. Assertion without a customer source, from the competitor's analyst — but it is the only stated mechanism by which CIEN hits 30% on VOLUME and misses on PRICE, and MS names the same risk independently.** ✅ **Corrected on the page: the Forum was in OTTAWA, not Calgary (a 09-15 JPM desk note had previewed "Calgary"); presenter list confirmed as Smith / Rothenstein / Gage / DiPerna / Hoffman / Graff.** ➤ Next dated: **ECOC 2026-09-22** (JPM hosting CIEN's Rothenstein and Gage) — the natural place to test whether optical component supply is loosening sooner than MS assumes. |

| 2026-09-15 | TXN | bear won — ⚠️ **PARSER DATE = THE OBSERVATION DATE, NOT AN EVENT DATE.** The bullet is a standing watch (*"watch whether BofA's defensive-analog expression rotates toward TXN"*), and it resolved AGAINST on the very day it was written: in *"US Semiconductors: State of the Union"* (2026-09-15, relayed via BofA TMT sales commentary) **Vivek Arya's five names expected to show the best resilience in a range-bound tape were [[ADI]], [[ON]], [[NVDA]], [[AMD]] and [[MRVL]] — TXN absent** — while the same analyst's **BUY / PO $370 (36x CY27E, 2026-07-22) stands unchanged.** ➤ **Scored bear on the NARROW POSITIONING TEST ONLY.** The same session was net-bullish on fundamentals: BofA lifted its CY30 industry TAM to **$3.2tn from $2.7tn** citing *"incrementally higher recovery in AUTO/INDUSTRIAL"*, Didier Scemama raised Automotive to **$60bn (+12% y/y) CY26E** on content not volume, and Rothschild's TMT desk gave the page **its first SUPPLY-SIDE bull leg** (mature-node rationing toward AI, STMicro MCU lead times beyond a year, 2027 capacity already tight). **Nothing on the page moved — no rating, PT, guide, GM or estimate.** ⚠️ No reason for the omission is stated in the material; do not infer one. **The forward watch stays OPEN — re-test at the Sept-quarter print.** |
| 2026-09-15 | TSEM | bull won — the BofA-hosted virtual fireside with President Dr. Racanelli happened and did the one job the page set for it: **the prepayment question was asked and answered by management** — *"not booked in the sense that we don't have POs for all of that volume, but we have customer commitments… forecast as support, and in some cases, also prepayments"* — confirming first-party the mechanism behind the 09-07 Gaetano *"hundreds of millions"* characterisation. Bonus: the page's first first-party unit-economics ladder (**NPO is not capped at eight lanes, so PIC die area scales ~linearly with lane count 16/18/32/36+, against only ~15-20% more area for 800G→1.6T**), 85% utilisation confirmed, and *"all our tier-one customers are prototyping multiple NPO implementations"*. ⚠️ **NOT a clean win: the magnitude of the prepayments is still undisclosed, and on the same call management handed the CPO bear case its best quote** — *"TSMC will become more of a competitor there because they control the ASIC… naturally, because of that, their share will increase"*, with the counter (customers welcome best-of-breed) unbacked by any contract, design win or cost comparison. **No rating, PT or estimate on the page moved.** ⚠️ The host bank is NOT named in the transcript (Notion AI machine transcript, unlabeled speakers); the BofA/Oliver Wong attribution comes from this page's own 09-07 catalyst line. |

Both are on the page. **What moved:** ① the **+70% FY28 revenue guide is *"a floor"***, given because *"bottoms-up demand runs ~2x supply"* and *"intended to stop a SHARE-LOSS PERCEPTION from impacting SUPPLIER ALLOCATION"* — i.e. the guide is a supply statement, not a demand ceiling; ② 🔴 **the gross-margin trough is CONTRACTED, not forecast** — *"pricing largely locked via LTAs (incl. [[SKHYNIX]]), so any significant DRAM move likely surfaces in FY29 rather than FY28"* (74% Q3 → 71-72% Q4 → 72-73% FY28), which retires the single biggest bear objection to the 08-26 print; ③ the **$/GW ladder $25bn Blackwell → $40bn Vera Rubin → $60-80bn+**, caveated to the FULL reference design (*"~$40bn effectively the MAX"* for a CPU/GPU-only hyperscaler); ④ the **~$105bn [[OPENAI]]/SB Energy guarantee decomposed** — SB Energy OWNS the site, NVDA guarantees the LEASE, exposure PEAKS at ~9 completed DCs then BURNS DOWN, no cash out absent default; ⑤ the six-partner backstop is *"an up-to-25% OPTION vs peers reportedly >80%"* resting on GPU fungibility — *"a defaulted site re-leases, an ASIC can't."* JPM adds the end-consumption concentration number the page lacked: **[[OPENAI]] + [[ANTHROPIC]] ≈ ~20% of NVDA's business TODAY on an END-CONSUMPTION basis → ~25% in FY28**, and *"neoclouds are more than ~50% of ACIE."* ⚠️ **One unsourced figure to quarantine: BofA's *"peers reportedly >80%"* is UNNAMED and is NOT an [[AVGO]] number.** ➤ The 08-26 print itself is already scored (bull won, re-scored 2026-08-27); this line closes the ACCESS event, not the print. |
| 2026-09-02 | HPE | bull won — **Every number in the bogey grid this page carried was cleared, several by wide margins, and the FY27 framework was raised a full quarter earlier than expected. The stock moved ~−1%, and the reason is a sibling, not a fundamental.** Against the JPM · Meyers survey grid logged 08-24: **revenue $12.2bn vs guide $11.8bn / BBG $11.85bn (+3.0%)**; **gross margin 40.4% vs 36.1% (+430bp)**; **EPS $1.11 vs guide $0.91 / BBG $0.93 (+19%)**; **FCF $1.60bn vs $0.93bn (+72%)**; **F4Q revenue guide $14.35bn vs $12.84bn (+11.8%)**; **F4Q EPS guide $1.17 vs $1.06 (+10%)**; **FY26 EPS $3.75-3.85 vs $3.35-3.45 (+12%)**. ✅ **The page's own 08-24 shape call was right and then some** — it had flagged that consensus embedded a +8% q/q step into F4Q; the actual guide is +17.6%. **Quality of the beat: Cloud & AI $9.0bn (GSe $8.8bn) with C&AI EBIT margin 17.0% against a low-to-mid-teens guide, on traditional server ORDERS +75% — a margin beat, not only a price beat.** Two net-new assets: a **$3.5bn hyperscaler inferencing deal awarded AFTER quarter-end (unnamed, not in backlog)** and a **warrant-linked gigawatt-scale [[ORCL]] networking agreement**. FY27 raised: **revenue growth 8-12% → 13-17%, operating margin 12-16% → 14-15%, EPS growth 12-16% → 16-20%**, with 75%+ of FCF committed to shareholders in F4Q. ⚠️ **Three qualifications kept, because they are why a beat this size did nothing:** (1) **Networking landed MID-GUIDE (+75% y/y against 73-78%) — and that is precisely the line MS's 08-24 call was conditioned on: *"IF NW beats expects, would expect strong T+1 re-rating."* The condition did not trigger**, though orders were a record +36% y/y; (2) **four desks independently scored HPE against [[DELL]] rather than against its own bar** — *"DELL remains the cleaner enterprise-hardware play"* (TMTB), *"more upside in DELL even after the move"* (Barclays), *"debates will remain just as they did post-DELL around the durability of margins, pricing, and demand"* (UBS); (3) **the price-vs-unit decomposition of the margin beat was not given, so the memory pass-through / pull-forward question is unresolved — identical to DELL the previous evening.** ⚠️ **Two data conflicts logged rather than averaged: JPM's desk marked the post-print move at −7.4% against ~−1% from Barclays, UBS and TMTB; and JPM's desk marked the FY27 revenue prior at 12-16% where GS says 8-12% and Barclays/TMTB imply a ~10% midpoint. The page adopts ~−1% and 8-12%.** |

| 2026-09-02 | AVGO | neutral — **The rarest outcome on this ledger: the numbers missed the bar, the call beat the thesis, and the stock fell. Scoring it either way would lose the information.** F3Q26 printed in line (revenue **$29.6bn** vs a Street spread of $29.2-29.5bn, **EPS $3.32** vs $3.22-3.24, **GM 75.0%**, **OpM 67.9%**), **AI semis $16.7bn (+221% y/y, +54% q/q)** beat every Street mark — though by anywhere from $0.6bn to $1.5bn depending on whose consensus you use (GS $16.1bn / Barclays $15.9bn / TMTB $15.9bn / UBS $15.2bn). The FQ4 guide was the first negative: **revenue $34.8bn** roughly in line but **operating margin 66.0% BELOW every desk mark (GS 67.5%/Street 66.8%, Barclays 66.5%, UBS 66.5%)** — a guide-down on the exact metric management had asked to be judged on (*"focus more on OPERATING margins, not GM"*). **The catalyst was written against the FY27 AI bar and that bar was MISSED by every measure: guide $115bn vs UBS model $130bn, buy-side bogey ">$135bn", Barclays desk "need at least $130bn", Street $121bn (Vital Knowledge) and Wells Fargo's Street-high $141.5bn.** ➤ **But the FY28 guide, which nobody had, BEAT the Street by ~28% ($230bn vs ~$180bn) with EPS ">$30" vs ~$25.80 — so the ramp did not shrink, it moved right by about a year on the Street's schedule, and management reframed the whole thing as "$350bn over 2 years" with "a high degree of confidence."** **Three genuine thesis wins on the call:** ① the [[GOOG]] share-loss fear was answered head-on (*"engagement has never been stronger"*, multi-tens-of-billions annually, **v8i shipping AHEAD of [[MEDIATEK]]'s v8T "which was initiated earlier"**) — Vital Knowledge: *"that doesn't seem to be the case"*; ② the constraint was relocated from demand to supply and itemised (**foundry, substrates, HBM, land/power/shell**), with **$115bn explicitly labelled a SECURED-SUPPLY number management will revise up** — a self-declared upgrade trigger; ③ the customer ladder was disclosed in gigawatts for the first time by any vendor (**[[ANTHROPIC]] 1/5/10GW and #1 from FY27; [[OPENAI]] 1.3GW/>5GW and #2; [[META]] MTIA 3GW through 2028**). ⚠️ **Four things stop this scoring bull:** (1) the FQ4 op-margin guide is below Street on management's own chosen metric; (2) the **$/GW arithmetic was asked directly and left unbridged — $20-30bn stated vs $11-12bn implied by Hock's own 10GW/20GW counts, a ~2x spread inside one answer**; (3) **the $29bn off-balance-sheet figure was CONFIRMED, not denied**, with future lab financing left open (*"tailored to labs and investor needs"*, *"case by case"*, *"A is going to IPO with a different credit rating"*); (4) **TPU v10 / customer-owned tooling — the value-capture question this wiki opened 08-26 — was not addressed at all**, and "never been stronger" does not answer it. **Tape: −2.3% to −4% in the post across four desks, with JPM's desk calling AVGO "a quite crowded net long" and Barclays' calling positioning "leaning short" on the same day — do not use either as evidence about fundamentals.** |

| 2026-09-01 | RDDT | neutral (NOT SCOREABLE — a CONFERENCE WEEK, not a binary event, and no primary on file) — the catalyst was **GS COMMUNACOPIA + TECHNOLOGY, San Francisco, week of 2026-09-01**, flagged off Goldman's own framing that its 08-30 r/bodega takeaways set up *"key debates into our Communacopia + Technology conference in San Francisco next week"* (GS · Eric Sheridan). **RDDT's high-water mark is 2026-08-30 — no session readout, transcript or desk relay from the conference is in the corpus, and the page ALREADY warned that the debates themselves were behind the note link and were not captured.** ⚠️ **Classification note: this is an ACCESS/CONFERENCE catalyst on a name whose thesis turns on a disclosed metric, so it can rarely resolve bull-vs-bear by itself — the two scoreable RDDT events are structural, not conversational: (1) THE DISCLOSURE CLIFF — Q3'26 is the LAST quarter Reddit reports the logged-in/logged-out DAUq split, which removes the bear case's core evidence WITHOUT resolving it; and (2) the GOOGLE/[[OPENAI]] DATA-LICENSING RENEWALS, where management's own Q2 language (*"Is there a world in which we don't do licensing? Well, I think the range of outcomes is wide"*) reads as expectation management against JPM's 2H framing of renewals at ~2-2.5x the prior money (Anmuth, 2026-07-14).** ➤ **If a Communacopia readout arrives, file it as evidence into those two debates; do not re-open this as a scored catalyst.** |
| 2026-09-01 | DELL | bull won — **The cleanest bull win on the ledger this quarter, and it won on the exact mechanism the page had underwritten since 08-14.** F2Q27 (AMC 16:05 ET) beat every line: revenue **$47.0bn** vs Street $44.9bn, **EPS $7.04** vs $4.91/$4.92, GM **21.1%** vs 17.3%, operating margin **12.6%** vs 9.3%; ISG **$31.8bn / 15.0% OM** vs Street $29.6bn / 10.8%; AI-server orders a record **$60.9bn** (vs $24.4bn in F1Q) into a **$95bn** backlog; traditional servers **+122%**. FY27 guided up twice — revenue **+$25bn to $192bn**, EPS **$17.90 → $25.50**, AI-server **$60bn → $74bn** (three desks agreeing to the decimal: Barclays desk · Rand; UBS sector sales · Ruple; GS · Murphy/Ng, 2026-09-01). **Scoreboard vs the bogeys this catalyst was set against (BofA · Wamsi Mohan, 08-31): all four cleared** — the AI triple ($15.6bn/$25bn/$60.6bn modelled vs $16.4bn/$60.9bn/$95bn actual), the FY27 raise (bar $171-175bn / ~$18.90 → actual $192bn / $25.50), the AI-server guide (bar *"toward $65bn"* → $74bn) and CSG margin (6.0% guide, BofA 6.3% → 7.6%). Management then supplied the mechanism the bulls lacked, and it is the reason this scores bull rather than neutral: CFO Kennedy attributes **just over 400bp** of the ISG rate expansion in the quarter and **over 650bp** in the full-year guide to **SCALE, not to a memory spread**, and guides the ISG rate UP sequentially *into* an AI mix that triples — which out-runs (rather than refutes) the AI-mix-dilution bear this page has carried since Bernstein's initiation. ⚠️ **Three qualifications, all kept because they are why the tape faded +10% → +6.6%:** (1) the bear immediately re-labelled the beat *"margin stacking on memory"*, and both sides now agree on the MECHANISM — the disagreement is only whether it is earnings power or a mean-reverting spread (JPM · Meyers, off-the-call, 09-01); (2) management **CONCEDED** *"a notion of inflation inside our growth"*, **refused the volume/price split**, and left JPM's customer-pushback question unanswered; (3) the FY28 comp was put to them directly by Melius against NVDA's ~+70% and **DECLINED** — so *"is F27 peak earnings?"* survives the call and stays open, resolving on external memory contract pricing rather than on a Dell disclosure. **Also settled by the management primary: the $49.0bn vs $46.76bn F3Q guide conflict (Barclays right, UBS wrong) and the 12.6%/12.9% operating-margin double-mark.** |

| 2026-09-01 | CRDO | bear won — **The page named the single falsifier in advance, it failed, and that one line was the whole move: the FY27 optical guide was NOT raised.** F1Q27 beat the guide and the Street — revenue **$479.0m** vs Street $473.0/$473.3m, GM **68.0%** in line, **EPS $1.20** vs $1.16/$1.17 — and the stock fell **−9.8%**. The catalyst was written as *"a revision anywhere in $650-750M clears Jefferies and misses JPM; a reiteration at $600M misses both"*: management **held at $600m**, against a **buy-side bar of $1bn** where *"the expectation was for at least a $100m raise to track that"*, and against JPM's own preview estimate-driver #1 of **$700m+** (JPM · Meyers off-the-call 20:24 ET; JPM · Cardoso preview, 09-01). Compounding it, management framed **AEC DOWN on its own call** — *"not a breakout"*, *"law of large numbers"* — which is Credo contesting its own Q4 FY26 *"core growth engine"* language, and the buy-side read it as deceleration rather than conservatism. The F2Q guide was the secondary insult: **$525-535m (mid $530m)** vs Street $519.8m/$526m, i.e. *"investors were looking for LDD upside (or HSD at a minimum) on the guide and instead CRDO guided 1% upside for F2Q"* (Barclays desk · Rand); **operating margin 48.2% vs Street 49.3%** was the one reported line that missed (UBS: *"maybe some nitpicking on the lower OPM"*). ⚠️ **Two things stop this being a thesis break rather than an expectations break, and both belong in the score:** (1) **Jefferies' 08-27 marks were exactly right on both the quarter ($480m) and the guide ($530m) — the Street's MODEL was fine, it was the BUY-SIDE BAR that was wrong**, which is the transferable lesson; (2) the second-hyperscaler binary did NOT resolve either way, and **two PIC design wins plus NPO wins were disclosed**, with JPM reading the feedback as *"more like disappointment than an interest actively to short the name."* **Client concentration and the optical ramp remain the two risks the buy-side cannot get comfortable with.** |

| 2026-09-01 | ASML | neutral — **A research-note supersession rather than a dated event, and the insertion calendar moved in BOTH directions, which is why it scores neutral rather than either way.** The catalyst as written was *"High-NA HVM insertion (Intel A14A ~2029; first Logic layer adoption at TSMC A14 ~2028)"*; Bernstein's High-NA primer (*"ASML: High-NA EUV Primer - The shrinking never stops"*, 2026-09-01) retires both legs at once. **Earlier than the wiki carried:** [[INTC]] is **ALREADY in HVM on 18A in 2026** — *"Specific Intel 18A layers have been dual-qualified on HNA with product shipping to customers at yields matched to the NXE platform"* — not A14A in 2029, with broader use at A14. **Later than the wiki carried:** [[TSM]] is **NOT a 2028/A14 adopter — it is ~2030 at A10**, alongside Samsung logic. **And the first BROAD adoption is reassigned to a different end-market entirely: DRAM at 1d in 2027, led by [[SAMSUNG]] and [[SKHYNIX]]**, with logic expanding gradually 2028-2030. ➤ **Net for ASML: no change to the direction and no change to any house number — the scoreable trigger was not resolved, it was re-specified and pushed forward.** The forward test Bernstein hands over is now explicit, and it is a MEMORY test rather than a logic one: *"insertion decisions and follow-on orders more likely to materialize in 2H26 and into 2027"* against a *"sizable low double-digit HNA backlog"*, with **a LARGER ORDER WAVE expected later** — so the tell that the second High-NA order wave is coming is **a DRAM insertion decision**. That is what replaces this catalyst on the page. Old values moved to the page's `## Changelog`. |
| 2026-09-01 | AAPL | neutral — **The transition executed exactly as pre-announced, so there is no information in the event itself — and every scoreable test it was set against is still ahead.** John Ternus succeeded Tim Cook effective 2026-09-01 (Cook's last day 2026-08-31; Bloomberg via MS TMT desk, 2026-08-31), on the timing and in the manner the page had carried since Barron's framed it in June. The catalyst's own stated watch items — *"whether the September product event is used to reset the Siri/AI timeline"* and how Ternus frames the AI stack that the foldable-2026 → glassy-2027 cadence depends on — **resolve at the 09-09 product event and at the late-October F4Q print, not here.** ⚠️ **Two live setups this hand-off now sits in front of, both already on the page and both unresolved:** (1) Bernstein's low bar — *"Siri AI (though most don't believe it will be significant until sometime in 2027)"* (Seidman, 08-31) — makes a materially capable Siri at launch the falsifier for the bear, with low expectations the base case; (2) MS's most falsifiable call on the page, that Apple's ~**3.5pts of guided September supply shortage** (~$3.5bn, implied to fall *entirely* on the Mac against TSMC checks showing no apparent A-series SoC shortage) is **"an overly-conservative Product revenue bar"** deliberately set to hand the incoming CEO a beat in late October (MS · Woodring, 08-03) — which resolves on one line, Sept-Q Mac revenue against MS's own **$8,724mn / 0% y/y**. **Against MS: the only product with a wait time in Jefferies' six-market checks was MacBook >16GB, and Gurman reported major MacBook Air shortages with new orders "not arriving until September."** ➤ **Score the succession neutral; the two real tests are dated 09-09 and late-Oct.** |
| 2026-08-31 | TXN | neutral — **CFO transition completed as scheduled and it was a governance milestone, not an event.** Rafael Lizardi's advisory period ended 2026-08-31; **Julie Knecht** (25-yr veteran, ex-CAO) became CFO effective 2026-08-01 (DB · Ross Seymore, briefing 2026-06-03). ⚠️ **One correction to the page's own framing: the Catalysts bullet said the Q2 print would be "the first call with new CFO Julie Knecht" (DB, 06-03), but the 07-22 Q2 print was in fact **Lizardi's last** — Knecht was effective 08-01, so her first call is the Q3 CY26 print (~late Oct 2026)** (Global Semis Daily · William Beavington, 2026-07-19; BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-07-20 both name it as Lizardi's final print). **Nothing on the page moved on or around 08-31 — the last TXN content is 08-21 (Arete initiation folded) and the 08-18 pricing rows.** DB was explicitly **neutral on the transition** and no house re-rated on it. The scoreable content of the quarter belonged to the print, not to the handover: Q2 beat on all three lines (rev $5.46bn vs $5.0-5.4bn guide, GM 61.4% vs St ~59.4%, EPS $2.14 vs St ~$1.92) with a **~24% capex MISS vs consensus ($514m vs est. $676m)** that cut directly against the MS Underweight capex-drag thesis. ➜ **Carry forward: Knecht's first call (Q3, ~late Oct) is the real event — it is where the capex-roll-off/FCF-per-share framing either survives a change of CFO voice or gets re-cut.** |

| 2026-08-27 | NVDA | bull won (narrow — this line closes BOTH broker recap calls flagged on this date: the UBS one delivered in full, the BofA one produced no readout at all) — **UBS's 08:30 ET earnings recap call with Tim Arcuri is comprehensively on the page and is the richest post-print source in the corpus after the company primary.** ✅ **Net constructive: C2027E/C2028E EPS raised $16.20/$19.81 → $17.16/$22.08 with the *"~10x EPS in the aftermarket — too cheap for the growth that is still in front of the company"* framing; plus the strongest available rebuttal to the "three customers are the whole company" bear leg — *"FQ2 customer concentration DECREASED q/q and y/y, with NVDA highlighting only ONE direct customer representing 16% of total revenue (or $15.4bn). This compares to 54% of revenue split between THREE direct customers in FQ1:27 and 39% between TWO in FQ2:26."*** ⚠️ **NARROW because the same call produced two new falsifiable BEAR constraints no other house put on the page: (1) THE ALLOCATION CONSTRAINT ON THE +70% GUIDE — *"NVDA is tracking to ship ~13GW this year (~7GW to hyperscalers and ~6GW to neoclouds) and NVDA's new C2027 guidance (including ASP increases) implies ~19-20GW. However, the emergence of [[SPCX]] (~6GW added in C2027) would seem to put NVDA in a HEAVY ALLOCATION SITUATION and leave LITTLE TO NO ROOM FOR SHIPMENTS TO HYPERSCALERS TO GROW AT ALL"* — a bull on numbers arguing the guide is internally crowded; and (2) the timing tell — NVDA *"has historically been surprisingly well correlated with y/y revenue growth… and it appears likely to have PEAKED IN FQ2."*** **Also net-new and worth keeping: ~8.4M GPUs in C2027 (from 8.1M in C2026 despite Rubin's higher TDP and ASP); CPU revenue ~$20bn C2026 → ~$40bn C2027 on ~4-5M units; LPX ~$32bn on ~1.6M units in C2027; FQ4 revenue >$130bn from supply-chain work against no company soft guide; and the INFERENCE (not a disclosure) that the customer reclassified out of ACIE into Hyperscale is SPCX at ~$13bn over six months — which, if right, flatters Hyperscale's +13% q/q and penalises ACIE's +25%, the OPPOSITE direction to the segment narrative.** ⚠️ **On commitments the call is one of four bases and they must not be netted: UBS's constructed $311bn (+115% q/q, explicitly excluding cloud commitments) and *"$366bn total future commitments"* sit alongside JPM's ~$350bn and the MS/JPM $119bn → $279bn FY2028+ figure. UBS's decomposition is the useful one — FY2027-dated commitments actually DECLINED to $92bn from $95bn, so all the growth is FY2028+.** 🔴 **THE GAP: NO READOUT EXISTS FROM THE BofA 08:00 EST post-print call with Vivek Arya. The page carries it only as a scheduled item (BofA sales, 2026-08-24) and no BofA-attributed 2026-08-27 content appears anywhere on it (08-27 attributions on file: UBS/Arcuri ×2, MS/Moore ×2, SIG/Rolland ×2, Jefferies/Curtis, JPM/Sur, Rothschild, corpus). Arya was on the earnings-call Q&A so a note almost certainly exists — route it in the next `/run-inbox`. Lower priority than the MRVL gap below, because BofA's pre-print poll is already the best-documented buy-side bar on the page.** |

However, the emergence of [[SPCX]] (~6GW added in C2027) would seem to put NVDA in a HEAVY ALLOCATION SITUATION and leave LITTLE TO NO ROOM FOR SHIPMENTS TO HYPERSCALERS TO GROW AT ALL"* — a bull on numbers arguing the guide is internally crowded; and (2) the timing tell — NVDA *"has historically been surprisingly well correlated with y/y revenue growth… and it appears likely to have PEAKED IN FQ2."*** **Also net-new and worth keeping: ~8.4M GPUs in C2027 (from 8.1M in C2026 despite Rubin's higher TDP and ASP); CPU revenue ~$20bn C2026 → ~$40bn C2027 on ~4-5M units; LPX ~$32bn on ~1.6M units in C2027; FQ4 revenue >$130bn from supply-chain work against no company soft guide; and the INFERENCE (not a disclosure) that the customer reclassified out of ACIE into Hyperscale is SPCX at ~$13bn over six months — which, if right, flatters Hyperscale's +13% q/q and penalises ACIE's +25%, the OPPOSITE direction to the segment narrative.** ⚠️ **On commitments the call is one of four bases and they must not be netted: UBS's constructed $311bn (+115% q/q, explicitly excluding cloud commitments) and *"$366bn total future commitments"* sit alongside JPM's ~$350bn and the MS/JPM $119bn → $279bn FY2028+ figure. UBS's decomposition is the useful one — FY2027-dated commitments actually DECLINED to $92bn from $95bn, so all the growth is FY2028+.** 🔴 **THE GAP: NO READOUT EXISTS FROM THE BofA 08:00 EST post-print call with Vivek Arya. The page carries it only as a scheduled item (BofA sales, 2026-08-24) and no BofA-attributed 2026-08-27 content appears anywhere on it (08-27 attributions on file: UBS/Arcuri ×2, MS/Moore ×2, SIG/Rolland ×2, Jefferies/Curtis, JPM/Sur, Rothschild, corpus). Arya was on the earnings-call Q&A so a note almost certainly exists — route it in the next `/run-inbox`. Lower priority than the MRVL gap below, because BofA's pre-print poll is already the best-documented buy-side bar on the page.** |

Any revision to that 22% revalues the K3 decision AND the late-2027 TrendForce oversupply call at once.** ⚠️ **PARSER NOTE: this bullet will re-flag as ⏰ Passed on every future rebuild unless the FY2029 date is written in a form the parser prefers over the 08-27 announcement date — same class of artifact as the GOOG 2026-08-20 entry. | 2026-08-26 | NVDA | bull won — **RE-SCORE, superseding the "NOT YET SCOREABLE" line filed against this same date on 2026-08-27. The corpus has caught up (`_meta/sources_through.json` NVDA now 2026-08-27; the company primary, the CFO Commentary and the 17-page call transcript are all on the page), so the print is now scored against the bars it was opened with.** 🔴 **REVENUE CLEARED EVERY BAR: Q2 FY27 revenue $96,221mn (+18% q/q, +106% y/y) against the company guide ~$91bn (+5.7%), the JPM buy-side survey $93.6bn, JPM research $94-95bn and the TMTB desk bogey ~$95bn — beat on all four; non-GAAP EPS $2.22 vs the $2.18-2.19 polls; GM 75.0% in line. Data Center $89.0bn (+117% y/y), ACIE $40bn (+25% q/q, +138% y/y).** 🔴 **THE FQ3 GUIDE OF $108.0bn ±2% SPLIT THE TWO BUY-SIDE POLLS EXACTLY AS THE 08-24 PRE-EVENT ENTRY SAID IT WOULD: +3.3% above Street ~$104.5bn, at the top of JPM research's $107-108bn, above the JPM survey ~$107bn — but ~1% BELOW BofA's $109bn, the highest buy-side bar. The "$110bn+" tail did not print, and JPM's stated reason held: wafer availability, not demand.** 🔴 **THE THING NOBODY HAD ON THE CARD IS THE FIRST FULL-YEAR-AHEAD GUIDE IN NVIDIA'S HISTORY — FY28 revenue +~70% y/y, framed explicitly as a SUPPLY number: *"This is a supply constrained outlook… Customers' forecasts point to our growth DOUBLING next year"* (Kress), with Jensen confirming *"at this moment we have supply for 70%… our demand is much higher than that."* ⚠️ It converts NVDA's own supply book into a public commitment so the chain builds to it — but it is also mechanically a demand-disclosure CEILING: any 2027 upside now reads as "supply came in better," never "demand surprised." Supply stays binding *"at least through the end of fiscal year 28."*** ⚠️⚠️ **THE ONE CLEAN NEGATIVE, AND IT IS WHAT THE BUY-SIDE WAS NOT POSITIONED FOR: the gross-margin floor was cut ~400bp, deliberately and all at once — *"EXTREME PRICING CONDITIONS IN MEMORY… the magnitude of the price increase has EXCEEDED OUR PRIOR EXPECTATIONS and are headed even higher into next year. As a result, WE ARE RESETTING EXPECTATIONS TODAY"* (Kress). Path: 74.0% ±50bp FQ3 (vs the 74.8% buy-side bogey — a MISS) → 71-72% FQ4 trough → 72-73% FY28 settle. That kills two marks this page carried 48 hours earlier: JPM Market Intelligence's *"nobody seems at all concerned the team can't maintain 75% GMs"* (08-24) and UBS/Arcuri's *"GM ~75% through C2027"* assumption, on which his ~$681bn C2027 revenue / ~$360bn FCF build rested. It also sits 100-200bp BELOW this page's own CY2027E BBG snapshot line of 74.0%.** ✅ **Scored BULL WON on the tape and on estimate direction, not merely on the beat: NVDA entered on a 7-session losing streak at ~−1 z-score of positioning (Vanda via Jefferies, 08-26) having traded DOWN the day after each of the last four prints; it traded +4-5% after hours and the streak broke. UBS/Arcuri took C2027E/C2028E EPS $16.20/$19.81 → $17.16/$22.08 (*"~10x EPS in the aftermarket — too cheap"*). The +70% FY28 guide beat the sell-side bar by ~25 points and the buy-side bar by ~6 — that gap is the whole explanation for the move.** ⚠️ **Three carry-forwards, all now dated and falsifiable: (1) the GM trough — checkpoint the FQ3 print 2026-11-17 for whether 74.0% holds; (2) DSO went to 60 days on *"extended payment terms for large purchases by certain investment grade customers"* — two more prints of rising DSO against decelerating growth breaks that explanation; (3) the FQ3 guide assumes ZERO China DC compute revenue and Kress states current Hopper shipments are *"DILUTIVE to corporate gross margins"* — so both directions of the China debate lose their asymmetry. New and unbudgeted: *"roughly a quarter of our business next year"* from balance-sheet-financed AI labs (~$50bn already invested) moves the concentration risk from CUSTOMER to COUNTERPARTY CREDIT. NVDA is a book long (`_data/book.json`, seed).** |

| 2026-08-24 | LITE | neutral (NOT AN OUTCOME — logged to close the flag on a bullet that was written explicitly as "NO INFERENCE DRAWN") — **a Form 144 (proposed sale of securities) was filed, timestamped 16:15 ET (Lumentum IR alert via Q4 Inc., 2026-08-24).** The page's own bullet already states the correct reading and it has not changed: **a Form 144 is a NOTICE OF INTENT by an affiliate to sell restricted/control stock — not a completed sale, not necessarily an insider, and it carries no signal about the business.** The alert does not disclose share count, seller or price. ⚠️ **It stays unscoreable until the underlying filing is pulled — that is the only action item, and it is a data-gathering task, not a thesis question.** The one reason it was logged at all is timing: it lands the same week the page carries an active NPO-deal catalyst ("could be signed in coming months", 2026-08-24 AM). ➤ **Do not let the coincidence become an inference in a later ingest: an affiliate sale notice adjacent to a bullish disclosure is not evidence about either.** |

Wells — but no PT, rating, estimate or scenario mark on the page was superseded by it. 🔴 **THE ONE GENUINELY NEW DISCLOSURE IS A DATED SCHEDULE SLIP, AND IT WAS ADDED AFTER THE RECORDING: "after this was recorded, Elon Musk indicated on X that the TOWER CATCH OF THE SHIP (Starship 2nd stage) would be DELAYED for a 'few months'. Exact reason unclear, but likely REGULATORY in our view."** Score it against management's own 2Q26 call guidance — *"assuming we receive regulatory approval to do so, we'll attempt to catch the ship with the tower on the next flight"* — i.e. **the reusability milestone moved out by "a few months" versus "the next flight," a checkable slip on the half of the story that is not AI.** ⚠️ **The second negative in the same note is competitive rather than operational, and it is the one with structural teeth: China's LandSpace landed the Zhuque-3 booster (Reuters, 2026-08-19), the SECOND Chinese orbital-class booster recovery IN SIX WEEKS** — logged by the same Overweight analyst. | 2026-08-20 | ADI | bull won (narrow — the access delivered incremental bull disclosure AND the print's first rating change, but it DOWNGRADED THE ATTRIBUTION of the gross-margin bull case) — **both dated post-print access events happened (BofA fireside with Head of IR Jeff Ambrosi hosted by Vivek Arya, 11:00 ET; UBS "ADI Earnings Recap Call," NY) and the catalyst was opened as "the venue where the GM-guide argument gets settled or extended" — it was EXTENDED, with the mechanism RE-ATTRIBUTED.** 🔴 **The rating change is the news: BERNSTEIN (Stacy Rasgon) UPGRADES TO OUTPERFORM, PT $430 → $465, FY27 EPS $16.77 vs Street $16.34, with "~$20 of EPS in FY28 as plausible"** — explicitly a risk/reward call rather than a fundamentals call (*"with the stock trading in the low-20s on FY27 EPS, he thinks the improving growth trajectory has finally made the risk/reward attractive"*). **Sixth house to raise a PT off this print and the FIRST to change a rating — and it lands at $465, BELOW the $480-500 cluster (JPM $500 · BofA $500 · GS $480 · MS $458), so the rating is the signal and the PT is mid-pack.** 🔴 **The most useful NEW NUMBER of the day quantifies the cycle-runway claim management only asserted on the call: excluding the ATE/A&D secular supercycles, ~70% OF SALES REMAIN BELOW NORMALISED CONSUMPTION — core Industrial ~10-15% below trend, Consumer below trend, Auto roughly AT trend. Demand signals STRENGTHENED over the last 90 days and customer restocking "remains immaterial"** (BofA · Arya, "IR call: Cyclical torque building beneath AI supercycle as GMs grind higher", Buy, PO $500 at 23x CY28E, px $371.63). **A clean falsifier was logged on the obvious pull-in risk: bookings linearity shows NO unusual activity, and extended lead times make buying ahead of the September increase difficult — so a pull-in showing up in the Oct-Q anyway would contradict this directly.** **Net-new and dated on the data-centre leg: 2028 named as "a potentially strong INFLECTION"; POWER is 50% of the SAM but ADI's share is LOW (greater upside than optical, where ADI already leads the control plane); and Empower's IVR performance has secured engagement with THREE LARGE HYPERSCALERS, with design decisions occurring in 2H26** — the first named count of Empower/IVR engagements on the page, the falsifier being whether any converts to a disclosed design win by the FQ4 call. 🔴 **AND THE REASON THIS IS NARROW RATHER THAN CLEAN — THE PRICING FRAMING IS A DOWNGRADE FROM HOW THE STREET READ IT ON 08-19 AND MUST BE LOGGED AS SUCH: "price hikes are intended to OFFSET INFLATION rather than monetize shortages or differentiation"** (higher foundry, OSAT and raw-material costs), **February increases primarily targeted the CHANNEL while the September round is BROAD-BASED, and contracted/quoted OEM business — roughly HALF of ADI — reprices through annual negotiations extending into March. Reconcile against 08-19: JPM had September pricing keeping GM "within range of ~74%" into FY27 and BofA had a path to high-70% GMs; the IR call says the DEFENCE of 74% is the pricing job, and that getting to ~75% is a MIX story (Industrial/Comms accretive, data centre lifting Comms above the corporate average, Defense accretive, ATE slightly DILUTIVE), not a pricing story. Direction unchanged; ATTRIBUTION changed — which matters because a mix-driven 75% is slower and less controllable than a price-driven one.** **GM detail: 74% is "largely sustainable at flat FQ1 revenue," with only modest fab-maintenance pressure.** ➤ **The successor test is dated and unchanged from the print's own post-mortem: the September price increase actually landing, plus consensus Communications FY27/FY28 (+30%/−5%) which JPM calls "quite light." The catalyst said "the single most valuable thing the day can produce is a DATED HBF roadmap." IT WAS NOT PRODUCED: first memory die taped out, samples in 2027, NOT in the revenue projections, NO COMMERCIAL TIMELINE (Meta joining the HBF consortium is the only net-new). The Jefferies desk called it *"relatively lackluster/disappointing — clearly seems like it's still relatively far away."* That is the third successive softening of the HBF framing this page has tracked (2H26 samples → 1H27 samples / mid-27 customer feedback per the MS NDR 06-22 → now "samples 2027, no commercial date"), and HBF stays exactly what Bernstein called it: un-modelled optionality.** ✅ **The OTHER named ask WAS delivered: KV-cache was sized for the first time since the Q2 FY26 call — ~1ZB persistent installed base by 2030, ~35% OF THE NAND MARKET — which supersedes this page's standing "75–100 incremental exabytes in 2027, doubling thereafter" as the live management framing.** ⚠️ **The loudest pushback on the day is the reason to score this as a MULTIPLE event rather than a thesis event: NO THROUGH-CYCLE MODEL — three years only, and only the good ones.** GS put the governing caveat plainly: NBMs *"could help mute industry cyclicality [but] we think this concept STILL NEEDS TO BE PROVEN — and it will take time to be fully reflected in the stock's multiple."* The Jefferies desk's positioning read is the honest version of the bull case: *"peak bearishness in memory is already past us… [but] a MORE SLOW-BURN (and healthy) recovery, rather than a V-shaped bounce… it will probably take a few more Q's of consistent execution… for the multiple expansion thesis to fully play out"* — and it attributes part of the move to mechanics rather than conversion: *"some [bears] likely were FORCED TO COVER THEIR SHORTS today."* 🔴 **The number to carry forward is the consensus gap: the top-line framework implies large upward revisions, because current consensus embeds y/y of +16% / −4% / −30% for FY28E/29E/30E — i.e. the Street models revenue DECLINING in FY29 and FY30 against a company guiding mid-to-high-teens growth. Whether that gap closes is the tradeable question now, and it is checkable every quarter.** ⚠️ **Read against the 2026-08-05 "bear won" entry eight days earlier: this does NOT reverse it. The 08-05 loss was on price and volume (mid-80s GM called a "fair return," Sept GM flat-to-down, FY27 sellable bits cut to mid-teens); the 08-13 win is on DURABILITY AND CAPITAL RETURN. Note the ~80% long-run GM in the new framework sits BELOW the 84.6% just printed — the company is guiding its own margin down to a level it calls sustainable, which is consistent with the peak-margin read, not a rebuttal of it. Both entries stand.** ⚠️ **Three follow-ons now inherit the catalyst (Jefferies desk, 08-13): (1) whether a through-cycle model is ever published; (2) an HBF commercialisation date; (3) whether FY29/FY30 consensus closes to the framework. Still open from 08-05 and untouched by the day: the SanDisk-vs-[[KIOXIA]] ASP gap ("modest" vs +25–30% q/q) — price discipline or share loss.** (Company Investor Day, 2026-08-13, per BofA · Wamsi Mohan (Buy, PO $2,500, px ref $1,547.99), GS · James Schneider (Buy, PT $2,200), Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini (Positive, PT $2,745, px ref $1,344.29) and Jefferies TMT desk · Rankie Wong (sales & trading; desk carries Buy, TP $1,750), all 2026-08-13; MS NDR · Ivan Donaldson 2026-06-22; Bernstein · Mark Newman 2026-06-29/07-09; BBG `_data/estimates.json` asof 2026-08-13.) |

| 2026-08-12 | NBIS | bull won — **the print cleared all three of the focus areas the catalyst was written against, and it re-rated the entire neocloud cohort on a disclosure nobody had asked for: PRICE IN $/MW/YEAR. Stock +34%.** Rev **$582.3M (+454% y/y)** vs Street $557M; **adj. EBITDA $236.2M vs $158M — a ~50% beat**; operating cash flow **+$2.246B**; total contract value **+4x q/q**. ✅ **Scoring BofA's (Tal Liani, Buy PO $280, relayed 2026-08-10) three named focus areas one by one, because that is what the bullet actually asked: (1) CAPACITY ACTIVATION / DC EXECUTION — year-end 2026 contracted power RAISED to 5 GW from the ">4 GW" of last quarter (the escalation series is disclosed: >1 GW Aug'25 → >2.5 GW Nov'25 → >3 GW Feb'26 → >4 GW May'26 → 5 GW), with the first-ever per-year deployment guide, "more than 1 GW PER YEAR… starting in 2027"; (2) CAPEX REQUIREMENTS — PP&E purchases ~$5.65B, ~10x revenue, but the FUNDING is now specified and it is not equity: >$9BN of upfront customer prepayments expected in 2026 (~40% of the $20–25bn capex guide), ~70% of Q2 deals carrying a prepayment covering 50–60% of associated capex, $40BN of contracted/committed backlog to borrow against, and the $775M facility confirmed by the company at SOFR+250bps; (3) EBITDA MARGIN TRAJECTORY — Nebius AI cloud adj. EBITDA margin 50% in the quarter (24% Q4'25 → 45% Q1'26 → 50% Q2'26), group 41% from 32%, with every opex ratio improving (total operating costs 130% of revenue, from 206%).** 🔴 **The load-bearing datapoint is the ACV/MW ladder, and it is the single most useful number this wiki has for modelling any neocloud: 2026 BASE capacity $12M/MW → Q2 deals >$20–25M/MW → short-duration (3–6 month) capacity $40–50M/MW, with payback compressing to "1 year and 10 months, DOWN FROM OUR TWO-TO-THREE YEAR PAYBACK PERIOD PREVIOUSLY." The auction is the cleanest shortage evidence on the page — "the auction result was a price 15% HIGHER THAN WE'VE EVER SEEN BEFORE, and 20% HIGHER THAN OUR PIPELINE FOR BLACKWELLS" — and management is now DELIBERATELY WITHHOLDING SUPPLY: "we could have sold out our planned capacity already today. However, we are choosing not to do so." That is a materially stronger statement than the Q1 "sold out" frame, and it is an un-hedged bet on prices continuing up.** ✅ **The Hedgeye MSFT-walk bear (~20% probability, resting on the must-deliver-or-terminate 60-day cure) is MATERIALLY WEAKENED, not removed: "to date, we've delivered ALL capacity tranches to Microsoft under the contract, including TWO TRANCHES OVER THE PAST MONTH." The mechanism Hedgeye described was future tranches, and the Vineland EXPANSION hearing (2026-08-17) is still live — score that separately.** ⚠️ **What did NOT go the bulls' way, and it is a real datapoint on a print this strong: the FY26 outlook was RE-ITERATED ONLY, not raised. Either conservatism or an admission that capacity TIMING (not demand) is the binding constraint — unresolved, and the Q3 print is the adjudicator. Three other bear items are quantified and survive: SBC +~600% to $102.5M and EXCLUDED from EBITDA; the ATM was TAPPED for 12.7m Class A shares at a $224 weighted-average price (~$2.8bn gross) with 12.3m shares still authorised — dilution actually taken, at a disclosed price; and D&A at 45% of revenue is the number the depreciation bears should be reading.** ⚠️ **Basis discipline: 5 GW is CONTRACTED power (land + power commitments), not active and not IT-load — do not compare it to [[CRWV]]'s ~1.5 GW ACTIVE / 4.2 GW contracted without saying which base. And the NBIS row in `_data/estimates.json` (asof 08-12) is CARRIED OVER FROM 08-11, so the $259.20 price and the CY2026 rev $3,228.6M / CY2027 rev $11,743.2M marks are PRE-PRINT — the exit ARR of $3.0bn alone roughly equals the full consensus 2026 year, and the $40bn backlog sits against a consensus CY2027 revenue of $11.74bn. Re-run `/wiki-consensus` before using any NBIS consensus number.** (NBIS Q2'26 letter to shareholders and the Bloomberg live transcript of the Q2'26 call, both 2026-08-12; Vital Knowledge · Adam Crisafulli 2026-08-12; 22V Research · Dauvin Peterson 2026-08-12; TMT Breakout EOD Wrap 2026-08-12; BofA · Tal Liani preview 2026-07-20 relayed 2026-08-10; Hedgeye · Felix Wang 2025-12-10; reconciliation-2026-08-12.) |
| 2026-08-11 | LITE | bull won — **every published bogey on this page was cleared and the stock still went nowhere on the night, which makes this the cleanest "expectations verdict, not a fundamental one" of the season — and the bull got paid one day late.** JunQ revenue **$1.006–1.010B** vs the ≥$1,003M bogey and the $960M–$1.01B guide ✅; non-GAAP **GM 50.4%** vs the ≥48.5% bogey and 48.5–48.8% Street, **+~600bps** ✅; non-GAAP **OM 36.6%** vs a 35–36% guide ✅; **EPS $3.23 vs $2.97** (+28c). 🔴 **The guide was the event and it split the bogey stack exactly where the pre-print work said it would: F1Q27 revenue $1.225–1.275B (mid $1.250B) vs Street $1.151–1.16B (~+8%), OM 39.5–40.5%, EPS $4.05–4.35 (mid $4.20) vs Street $3.58–3.61 (~+16–18%) — CLEARING UBS ($1.15–1.20B) and MS ($1.175–1.20B), MEETING the Jefferies $1.25B+ bar at the midpoint only, and MISSING the $1.3B intraday whisper. Tape: −0.4% to −0.7% after hours.** ✅ **MS had explicitly named pulling the $1.25B target model forward into the September quarter as the BULL outcome and called it "unlikely to see this quarter" — LITE did exactly that, "reaching our target model MORE THAN A QUARTER AHEAD OF SCHEDULE" (Hurlston).** ✅ **The pre-print buy-side expert call (Papa Sylla, relayed 2026-08-11) was right about the MECHANISM before the fact — "1.15 might not be good enough because that's where the street is… I think what I heard is like 1.3" — and JPM's desk gave the cleanest post-mortem sentence: "the tepid response feels like an EXPECTATIONS PROBLEM… by the end of the day today folks were saying the company had to do $1.3b in revenue guide to work… somewhat reminiscent of the TXN print," with the EPS beat 13c ahead of its Buyside Bars and GM 610bps ahead of consensus.** 🔴 **What converts this from neutral to bull won is the 08-12 follow-through, and it resolved the page's #1 open question — MARGIN DURABILITY THROUGH THE TRANSCEIVER RAMP — in the bulls' favour: management raised the OUT-YEAR OPERATING-MARGIN TARGET to 40–44% from 38–42% (the first move in the long-term margin frame since the March-2026 Analyst Day), said NPO is ADDITIVE TAM ramping late 2027 rather than a CPO substitute, and pushed back directly on the China/InP-capacity bear ("no impact from newly announced Chinese InP capacity," pricing UP on CW lasers on superior yields). Every house raised numbers; three raised PTs — JPM OW $1,165 → $1,280, MS EW $900 → $1,000, Jefferies Buy $1,200, Barclays OW $1,000 unchanged, R&Co Redburn Buy reiterated. Price $886.35 (BBG, 2026-08-10) → $932.47 (BBG `_data/estimates.json`, fresh 08-12) = +5.2% across the two days.** ⚠️ **The bear that survived is a margin-SHAPE bear, not a demand bear, and it is now dated: Jefferies — "the steep ramp of that lower-margin [Cloud Light] revenue likely SOFTENS THE POTENTIAL FOR MARGIN EXPANSION OVER THE NTM before the UHP laser opportunity for scale-up begins to unfold more meaningfully in 2H27." I.e. the dilution window is the next ~12 months and the offset is 2H27. Two more items unresolved: NO formal NPO deal announcement (MS: announcements "may not be made for a couple of quarters," with no clear catalyst "until the fall and into OFC next year"), and OCS on track for its first triple-digit revenue quarter in F1Q but with NO upside to the $400M figure for 2H26 — so the page's own "OCS ≥$400M across Sept+Dec" bogey was MET, not beaten.** 🆕 **Two net-new competitive datapoints worth more than the quarter: Jefferies was surprised by "the view that they will win MAJORITY SHARE AT GOOGLE in C27" on OCS — a step up from the "three customers / sole-source-ish" framing this page carried; and on CPO, "while NVDA's plans for scale up deployments may push to 1H 2028, LITE's timeline has not changed, with ultra-high power laser shipments still expected to begin in 2H27" — the customer may slip while the supplier ships on time, which DECOUPLES LITE's revenue timing from the NVDA deployment date the whole CPO debate has been anchored on.** ⚠️ **Two process notes. (1) The catalyst bullet labelled the forward guide the "DecQ guide" while quoting the UBS $1,150–1,200M SEPTEMBER-quarter bogey — a fiscal-label slip in the bullet, not in the numbers; the event was the F1Q27 (September) guide. (2) The print supports the reconciliation-2026-08-12 finding that the HOUSE MODEL's 2026 leg is too low: house CY2026 rev $4.2bn / EPS $12.81 vs consensus $4.50bn / $14.30 (−7% / −10%), against a company that just guided a single quarter to $1.25bn and hit its target model early. The house's above-Street 2027 leg ($8.1bn / $30.02 vs $7.86bn / $27.40) is the part worth keeping; the 2026 leg should be re-marked.** (LITE Q4FY26 release and call, 2026-08-11; JPM · Joseph Cardoso and Joshua Meyers, Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, MS · Meta Marshall, Barclays · Tom O'Malley and R&Co Redburn · Mike Harrison, all 2026-08-11/12; UBS · Robert Ruple and Vital Knowledge · Adam Crisafulli, 2026-08-11; buy-side expert call · Papa Sylla, relayed 2026-08-11; BBG `_data/estimates.json` asof 2026-08-12; reconciliation-2026-08-12.) |
| 2026-08-11 | CRWV | bull won — **the operating bogey this bullet was written against was not just cleared, it was raised: BofA's (Tal Liani, Buy PO $140) test was active power "~1.0 GW → 1.7 GW by year-end," and CRWV printed 1.5 GW ACTIVE at Q2 (+~500 MW q/q, the largest add in its history, with >300 MW in June alone — "June ITSELF LARGER THAN ANY FULL-QUARTER IN OUR HISTORY") and then GUIDED THE YEAR-END NUMBER UP to >1.85 GW from >1.7 GW. Stock +10% after hours on 08-11 and +19% on 08-12.** ✅ **The metric investors said mattered most was the one that beat hardest: adj. operating margin ~5.0% ($128M) against a ~3% bogey, a $30–90M guide and Street ~$66M. Adj. EBITDA $1.51B / 59% vs $1.42–1.43B / ~56%. Revenue $2.57–2.58B was merely in line. Contracted power ~3.7 GW at quarter-end and 4.2 GW as of 08-11; construction in progress $11.9B from $9.6B q/q, which Agrawal flags explicitly as the forward indicator.** 🔴 **The datapoint that re-rated the cohort was not in the release and came out on the call: NEW CONTRACTS SIGNED IN Q2 CARRY PRICE INCREASES OF ~25% ON AN APPLES-TO-APPLES BASIS ACROSS SKUs, and CONTRIBUTION MARGINS 5–10 POINTS ABOVE prior contracts (i.e. roughly mid-20s ➜ ~30–35%) — attributed by management PRIMARILY to demand and customer ROI, with component cost pass-through only SECONDARY (JPM · Samik Chatterjee, 2026-08-12; DB · Brad Zelnick, 2026-08-12). If that causality holds it is margin, not cost recovery. The second-most-quoted line is a residual-value datapoint rather than a pricing one: an A100 contract — a SKU introduced in 2020 — signed "at an attractive price" EXTENDING INTO 2029, i.e. a ~9-year monetised life on the oldest generation in the fleet, which is the strongest evidence yet against the depreciation/obsolescence bear this page carries.** 🔴 **The bear did not go away, and this print handed it its exhibit — which no desk note carried: Q2 INTEREST EXPENSE $640M vs $267M a year ago (+140%), GUIDED TO $860–940M in Q3, against Q2 adj. operating income of $128M. Interest is running ~5x operating profit, and adj. operating income actually FELL year-over-year ($128M vs $200M in Q2'25) even as it rose sequentially from $21M — the "operating leverage inflection" is a SEQUENTIAL story, not a y/y one. Net loss $(626)M. The direct offset is also management's own number: ~$18B raised in Q2 alone (>$32B cumulative) and the weighted-average cost of debt down ALMOST 300bps over the past year ≈ $1.1B of annualised interest saving — the quantified answer to the Rothschild/Bernstein "structurally disadvantaged by fixed debt" bear and to the SemiAnalysis 5.62%→10% funding-cost sensitivity.** ⚠️ **Three things went the other way and should be logged as such: (1) BACKLOG $104.2B against Cantor's $125–130B bull bogey — a ~$21B miss on the reported line, reconciled only by ~$25B of net-new commitments signed in early Q3 and excluded from the cut-off, plus a first disclosure of backlog QUALITY (>50% already attached to contracts where delivery has commenced, guided to >two-thirds by year-end); (2) Q3 PF OPERATING MARGIN GUIDED TO 6.5% at the midpoint vs 7.3% consensus, attributed to capacity landing late in the quarter — this is precisely the line JPM's Neutral is written against (7.0% vs 7.5% consensus), so it is now the cleanest falsifiable item on the page; (3) adj. capex $9.4B ABOVE the $7–9B guide, FY26 raised to $35–39B from $31–35B (DB reads the raise as acceleration of assets placed into service, NOT a change in cost to deliver).** ⚠️ **Management DODGED the one question the residual-value debate turns on: asked how much of the installed base comes up for renewal, Agrawal gave no number and no schedule — only that it is "a VERY LIMITED PART of our fleet." The DB 50% vs company 75% recontracting-ASP debate stays OPEN. And note the rating spread did not move: JPM Neutral $110 → $120 and Barclays EW $90 → $105 raised PTs most while staying Neutral/EW; DB's bull PT of $150 was UNCHANGED, and Bernstein (Tyler Seidman, 08-12) explicitly separates "L/T headwinds persist" from the positive near-term read-through.** ⚠️ **Consensus caveat: CRWV's row in `_data/estimates.json` (asof 08-12) is CARRIED OVER FROM 08-11, so the $107.73 price is the 08-11 close and pre-dates the +19% day. CRWV is loss-making at the consensus line (CY2026 EPS −$3.86), so the divergence vs the disclosed backlog is a REVENUE-RECOGNITION-TIMING question, not an earnings-power one.** (CRWV Q2'26 release and presentation, 2026-08-11; Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT of the Q2 2026 earnings call — Michael Intrator / Nitin Agrawal — 2026-08-11; JPM · Samik Chatterjee, DB · Brad Zelnick, Barclays · Raimo Lenschow and Bernstein · Tyler Seidman, all 2026-08-12; Barclays · Lenschow and UBS · Robert Ruple first takes, Vital Knowledge · Adam Crisafulli and TMTB EOD Wrap, all 2026-08-11; BofA · Tal Liani relayed 2026-08-10; reconciliation-2026-08-12.) |
| 2026-08-06 | SPCX | bull won — **the biggest single supply event on the page landed and the stock went UP 23% on the week: 900mn+ shares unlocked Thursday 08-06, increasing float by almost 150%, and "heavy pre-positioning meant the stock RALLIED THROUGH IT" (desk wrap via briefing, 2026-08-10).** **The price record is hard: PX_LAST $115.81 pre-print (08-04), $111.78 intraday on the unlock date itself (BBG live pull logged in reconciliation-2026-08-05), and $127.385 on 08-07 (BBG, `estimates.json` asof 2026-08-07) — i.e. +14% from the unlock-day mark and +10% through the print, on a week the desks scored +23%.** ✅ **22V (Dauvin Peterson, 08-04) and DB (08-03) both scored RIGHT and MS (Adam Jonas, 07-28) scored WRONG on this tranche: 22V's absorption arithmetic — 911mn unlocking shares vs a 555mn float, but only ~20% (~182mn) genuinely for sale, i.e. ~2-3 days of volume, plus short covering and September Nasdaq-rebalance positioning — is what happened; DB's "clearing event, not a persistent overhang" is the correct frame. Independent corroboration from a different asset class: Jefferies (Adam Panzer, 08-10) upgraded Alts to Bullish citing that "Retail flows and SPCX 1x fears have STABILIZED."** 🔴 **But the ATTRIBUTION is not the lockup and this must not be logged as a lockup-absorption win alone — the rally was driven by the compute reset the print produced, and the lockup simply failed to stop it: nameplate 1.4GW exiting Q2 → >2GW exiting 2026 → "closer to 10GW than 5GW" cumulative by YE27; $15.8bn of the $18.4bn Q2 capex into AI compute with the next two quarters guided similar (>$60bn annualised); NVIDIA exclusivity; $6.7bn of incremental cloud services contracted in the first weeks of Q3, ramping from October. SemiAnalysis then published a bottom-up defence of the 10GW number on 08-07 ("we believe THE NUMBER IS REAL") one day after the unlock, and NVDA rose 4% on the SPCX endorsement (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, 08-10: "SPCX Earnings Reads Bullish for NVDA/CRDO").** ⚠️ **THE OVERHANG IS NOT CLEARED — only the date is. This page's own "nine-week drip" bullet (Capstone internal research synthesis · Daniel Grozdea, 2026-08-04) says the scheme is 8-9 tranches, not one: ~7% more on 2026-08-21, further tranches 2026-09-10 and 2026-09-25 taking ~30% OF SHARES TRADABLE BY SEP 25, then ~4bn shares through Jan 2027 and Musk personally mid-2027. On the page's own logic DB's clearing event resolves in LATE SEPTEMBER, and Sep 25 is simultaneously the peak-supply date and the peak-passive-demand date (22V, 07-08: ~30% tradable is the trigger for SPCX moving from float-adjusted to FULL market-cap weight in the Nasdaq-100). ⚠️ NONE of the Aug-21 / Sep-10 / Sep-25 tranches carries an ISO date in a `## Catalysts` bullet, so the calendar will NEVER flag them — see the calendar-gap flag in the 2026-08-10 wiki-catalyst output.** ⚠️ **Two counterweights to carry into the next tranche, both dated AFTER the unlock: Bernstein (Tyler Seidman, 08-10) says AAPL/SPCX/TSLA "are becoming the less-favoured megacaps" with MSFT the year-end long — the relative bid weakened in the same week the stock rose 23%; and the cash line is untouched by any of this (1H26 FCF ≈ −$31bn; Q3/Q4 capex guided "similar to Q2"; consensus Q3 capex still sits ~41% BELOW the guide management just gave, per reconciliation-2026-08-05). That is the direct answer to the "peak growth quarter" objection UBS said was keeping investors out — and it is now the load-bearing assumption inside every raised PT on the page. Hold management to it each of the next three prints.** ⚠️ **Positioning is most of the move and should be logged as such: TMTB — "stock was SHORTED going in on the back of weaker 3p checks and the 'AI loser' narrative… just another sign of how much positioning can play in a big move." The re-rate has now consumed the sentiment gap that made the catch-up trade likely, so the next version of this question is harder.** ⚠️ **Two legs deliberately left open: (1) moat replicability — management's own answer on the call was that solutions are "designed to be ported" and it "must continue to earn business," a concession rather than a moat claim (GS, 08-03), and three independent sources in 48 hours described the ontology/FDE differentiator as replicable; (2) FCF/gross-margin quality — GM slipped to 86.3% vs 86.9% expected and MS's desk reports investors citing "lighter FCF" as the reason the stock did not move MORE (MS TMT desk · Tom Wigg, 08-04).** 🔴 **Process flag: the page has now lost its only captured Hold/Sell, so treat the unanimity as a positioning fact, not as confirmation.** (DB 2026-08-04; UBS 2026-08-03; GS 2026-08-03; MS desk 2026-08-04.) |

The bull leg it was meant to test is therefore untested, and this entry exists only so the calendar stops re-flagging a date that has passed.** **What the page DOES carry as the bar to score it against, from UBS (Sunny Lin et al., 2026-07-27): Ibiden announced a ¥220.0bn investment for its Gama plant in February 2026, with EMIB-T mass production scheduled to begin late 2027 and a more meaningful ramp in 2028 — and UBS's checks say "demand from Google TPU programs alone could largely absorb the planned capacity, increasing the likelihood of ADDITIONAL capex announcements as demand visibility improves." Read plainly: Intel's 2029-30 EMIB-T volume is gated on Ibiden's NEXT capex decision, not this one — so the specific thing to look for is an incremental announcement beyond the ¥220bn Gama tranche, and Unimicron (second source, "yields remain below optimal") is not a substitute. UBS also names TCB die-bonder demand (K&S, ASMPT) as the leading indicator of EMIB-T capacity expansion, which is a checkable alternative if Ibiden's own disclosure stays thin.** ⚠️ **Process note, and it is the second time in two days on this page: the 2026-08-03 INTC entry logged the same failure mode from the other direction — the parser dating a MONITORING bullet to the date of the note that created it. Here the date is real but the source is a third party this wiki does not systematically ingest. Action for /run-inbox or /wiki-ingest, not for this task: either fold in Ibiden's 08-04 result, or re-date the bullet to Ibiden's NEXT reporting date so it flags when there is something to read.** (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur, 2026-08-03; UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri et al., 2026-07-27.) |
| 2026-08-04 | AMD | bear won — **on expectations and on the tape, not on the thesis, and every desk says so explicitly.** Against the catalyst as written (the $11.2B guide + the Q3→Q4 gross-margin trajectory with MI450 mix-in): revenue **$11.54bn** beat both the guide and the $11.31bn Street **but missed the ~$12bn buy-side bogey**; GM **56.2%**, OM 26.8%, EPS **$1.66** vs $1.61-1.62. **Q3 guided $13.0bn ±$300m vs Street $12.5-12.6bn but vs bogeys of ~$13.2-13.5bn (Barclays heard $13.25-13.50bn, Jefferies $13.4bn); GM ~56%.** Segments: **DC $6.72bn vs $6.50-6.60bn, Client $3.06bn vs $3.00bn, Gaming $779m vs $791-814m (a miss), Embedded $977m in line. Stock −7% to −9%.** 🔴 **The catalyst's second clause resolved AGAINST the bulls and is the cleanest bear datapoint: gross margin was the ONLY guided metric below consensus, and it is guided FLAT q/q on a +13% sequential revenue step — i.e. the MI450 mix-in shows up as NO margin expansion. Corroborated from three directions on the record: Jean Hu's "data center AI… gross margin slightly below corporate average," management's admission that MI455X/Helios yields "will improve in the next few quarters" on "a highly complex product," and the Q3 guide itself.** **Why this is an expectations miss rather than a thesis break — GS: "we expect the stock to trade lower following a slightly stronger quarter and guidance, set against VERY HIGH EXPECTATIONS." Barclays: "don't think a lot wrong here on the bull thesis but… these numbers lean a little light for a CROWDED LONG name… I would expect there to be DIP BUYERS." Jefferies: "most are categorizing this as an EXPECTS MISS RATHER THAN A THESIS BREAK… sellside #'s clearly going up after this print, but hearing BUYSIDE #'s MOSTLY UNCHANGED OR SLIGHTLY TRIMMED." TMTB: "nothing here changes the bull debate as narrative centered around '27 and beyond."** **The real content was a 2027 guide-up in words rather than numbers, which is exactly why it did not clear the bar: total DC revenue growth "well over" +100% y/y in 2027 against sell-side consensus at +88% — but buy-side "settling in at around ~150% y/y, with some as high as 175-200%." ★ On the call C.J. Muse (Cantor) put ~$30bn of 2027 Instinct revenue to management and Lisa Su answered "your data center AI number is probably too low" — so consensus is the LOW anchor, and the disagreement is buy-side-vs-management, not Street-vs-management.** TAMs raised again (DCAI accelerator +45%/yr to $1.4trn by 2030; server CPU +50%/yr to $220bn), and management expects revenue growth "substantially above" the prior +35% long-term target and to "significantly exceed" the $20 long-term EPS target. ⚠️ **Two forward items worth carrying: consensus Q4 embeds a ~$2.7bn sequential revenue add (+20.9% q/q) underwritten by adjectives only — the weakest link in the AMD stack; and Jefferies has begun asking whether [[INTC]] is the better relative trade over the next few months (same CPU exposure, foundry-announcement optionality, less crowded positioning) against the EMIB-T datapoints landing the same week.** (GS · James Schneider, Barclays desk · Jeffrey Rand, Jefferies · Rankie Wong, TMTB EOD Wrap, BBG live transcript — all 2026-08-04.) |
| 2026-08-04 | ALAB | bull won on the numbers, unresolved on the tape — **and the gap between the two is the whole entry.** The catalyst asked for the $355–365M guide, the Scorpio X ramp, and confirmation Scorpio becomes #1 product line by Q4. **Revenue $392m** vs Street $361m and **above the high end of the guide**; non-GAAP **GM 73.7%** vs Street 73.0% (the ~200bps Amazon-warrant drag landed better than feared); **EPS $0.80 vs $0.69**. **The guide is the story: Q3 revenue $550m vs Street $417m and vs buy-side bogeys of ~$475m — i.e. +32% above consensus, +16% above the bogey, and +40% q/q — with non-GAAP EPS $1.185 vs Street $0.81 (~46% above).** ⚠️ **And the stock reversed from +8% to −2% intraday, which neither covering desk can explain. Barclays · Jeffrey Rand: "these numbers are CLEARLY ABOVE what I was hearing from the buyside… I was hearing like MID-TEENS revenue raise for 3Q and ALAB guided 32% ABOVE… however, I think more investors had SHIFTED POSITIVE the name into the print." TMTB: "NOT SURE WHY THE STOCK SELLING OFF FROM +8% TO −2%." Candidate explanations, all UNVERIFIED at time of logging: profit-taking in the most crowded scale-up long after MS's 40% PT raise the prior day; something on the call about Q4/2027 shape; or the GM step to 72% read as the start of a trend. Resolving this from the transcript / the 08-05 notes is the top open item on the page.** **Scoring the pre-print calls: MS (08-03) said "Scorpio ramp supports another BEAT AND RAISE," modelled Q3 +12.0% q/q and flagged the SepQ guide as a "likely upside surprise" — ✅ direction right, but its own +12% q/q model was a fraction of the +40% ALAB guided, so EVEN THE BULL WAS SHORT OF IT. The Trainium 3 Scorpio-X ramp is arriving faster and larger than the sell-side's own scale-up models, which is the content-per-XPU thesis landing in a single quarter.** ⚠️ **The blowout did not answer — and arguably worsened — the question that sets the multiple: diversification beyond Amazon. MS's framing stands: "beyond the quarter, the key debate is the DURABILITY AND BREADTH OF GROWTH AFTER THE CURRENT SCORPIO CYCLE," and a Trainium-driven +40% q/q guide makes ALAB MORE concentrated, not less. Two of MS's three checklist items are still open (design-win momentum, diversification); only gross margin was answered. Watch the Q2 10-Q concentration table against Q1's A 29% / B 21% / C 16% / D 12% / E 12%, and whether the "two additional major hyperscalers" on Scorpio P (guided late-2026) convert to disclosed revenue.** (Barclays desk · Jeffrey Rand, 2026-08-04; TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-04; MS, 2026-08-03.) |

| 2026-08-03 | ON | **split decision, bull won on the tape / bear won on the mechanism** — the 08-01 UBS-vs-08-02 Jefferies argument resolved in opposite directions on the two things it disagreed about. **Jefferies (Blayne Curtis) was right on the OUTCOME**: it called an explicit "BEAT & RAISE with revenue growth guided to HSD vs Consensus 5%," and the print delivered rev $1.60bn (Street $1.59bn), GM 39.3% (39.1%), EPS $0.74 ($0.71) and a Q3 guide above the Street on all three lines ($1.65-1.75bn / 40-42% / $0.81-0.93), stock +4% AH. **But Jefferies was wrong on the MECHANISM, and the mechanism was its entire argument**: the case rested on third-party auto/industrial evidence — "results from NXPI and TXN suggest the demand backdrop for Autos is IMPROVING while the Industrial recovery continues to BROADEN OUT" — and ON's own segments did the opposite. **Auto $781.3m, −2% q/q and BELOW Street $802.4m; Industrial $422.7m, +1% q/q and below Street $440.3m.** JPM called auto "head-scratchingly down 2% sequentially… a surprise given what TXN reported." **The entire beat sat in "Other" ($399.5m vs $332.8m) — the AI/compute line — plus gross margin.** **UBS (Arcuri) was wrong on the stock and right on the franchise**: it carried Neutral with the lowest sentiment score in its whole preview book (6/10) and the ON-specific flag that "our intra-quarter distributor checks suggest ON CONTINUED TO LOSE PRICING SHARE WHILE BUILDING UNIT INVENTORIES during Q2" — a claim Jefferies never rebutted and simply omitted. That flag is untouched by this print and arguably supported by it. UBS conceded the stock on positioning grounds itself: "not a blowout… something tells me there was a bit more SHORT-CROWDING here than I probably appreciated." **Two structural raises to carry forward: management moved AI-DC from "double in 2026" to "MORE THAN double," flagged an "expanded role in NVIDIA MGX," and rebuilt the TAM from $64bn-by-2029 to $213bn-by-2030 (AI-DC $12.2bn/40% CAGR → $47.5bn/72% CAGR, plus a new $14bn AD&S TAM).** **The generalisable lesson: peer read-through is not a substitute for company-specific channel work.** Both houses agreed on the setup (−40% from the $135 June peak, ~14x C28 EPS of $5.70) and the setup is what paid — not the thesis. GS reiterated Neutral / PT $95 and called a positive read-across for ADI and NXPI; UBS downgraded NXPI to Neutral the same day, so that read-across is already contested. (All marks 2026-08-03.) |
