Revenue $805M crushed both the $715-725M guide and Street $730M (a ~$80-90M beat vs guide, well above the "ordinary $50-60M beat" JPM framed as the bar), and Q3 guided $860-870M vs Street $830M, clearing JPM's "must guide 3Q above Street by ~$10-20M" test. The stock fell 8-13% anyway, because US LOGGED-IN DAUq came in at 23.1M vs Street 23.4M, DOWN ~100K q/q, with logged-out down ~200K q/q — against Bernstein's pre-print framing that "24M will be enough this quarter." Management conceded the mechanism in the shareholder letter: "Search referrals were choppy in the quarter, and traffic was more volatile later in the quarter … our visibility in referral traffic remains low," and repeated on the call that Search was a headwind — which the Street reads straight through to Google AI Overviews cannibalising referral traffic (MS Wigg: "feeds into the narrative that GOOGL is impacting traffic + top-of-funnel users"; independently, AI Overviews now appear on almost half of Google searches). Two structural additions: (1) this was the LAST quarter Reddit discloses the logged-in/logged-out split, so the bear case's core evidence disappears next quarter without the debate being settled; (2) on data licensing — the Google/OpenAI renewals that were supposed to be the 2H catalyst — management went materially softer than any prior framing: "Is there a world in which we don't do licensing? Well, I think the range of outcomes is wide." Confirms the standing page thesis that this equity trades on users, not revenue. |
| 2026-07-30 | AMZN | bull won — Q2 FY26 cleared every bogey that mattered and answered the ROI question the July de-rating was built on. Rev $200.6B (+20%) vs Street $197B and guide $194-199B; OI $27.5B vs Street $23.6B and vs its own $20-24B guide; AWS +36.7% y/y (37% FXN), FASTEST IN 18 QUARTERS, vs a ~35% buy-side bogey and >500bp above consensus, with AWS operating margin EXPANDING to 39.4% (~600bp above plan) while capex ripped. Backlog $364B → $496B in one quarter. Two new scaling disclosures: the AWS AI business exceeded a >$25B annual run-rate AND the chips business separately exceeded >$25B, both triple-digit % y/y — so AI run-rate stepped ~$15B → ~$25B q/q. Most important for the whole complex: management gave the season's only quantified AI-capex payback framework — "a little less than three years to break even" on servers/networking against a >=5-6 year useful life with most AI capacity contracted for >=5-year terms, off 30+ year data-center shells yielding "at least five to six generations of server economics," with the server half explicitly demand-gated ("if the demand isn't there, we won't spend the capital"). Demand exceeds supply for 2026 AND 2027, 2028 demand "striking," AWS "can reach a trillion dollars." Stock +8-10% AH and the supply chain rallied with it (ALAB/MRVL/CRDO +3-4%). Two real qualifiers, not cosmetic: (1) FCF was -$9B vs consensus -$1B (OCF $45.3B less $54.2B P&E) — Vital Knowledge is right that this "speaks to the cash pressures facing hyperscalers"; (2) FY26 capex went ~$200B → ~$220B and the increment was attributed EXPLICITLY to higher memory costs, i.e. ~$20B of extra spend buying no incremental compute. Q3 revenue guide of $195-202B sits below Street ~$204B but is Prime-Day-timing optical (~400bp, and adjusting back gives $204-209B). Note the contrast that made this a bull outcome rather than a mixed one: same 48 hours, MSFT held capex and rallied, AMZN RAISED capex and still rallied, META narrowed capex and fell 8-10% — the discriminator was visible accelerating cloud revenue alongside the spend, not the spend level. |
| 2026-07-30 | AAPL | bear won — the anti-AI safe-haven trade broke on the one input Apple does not control. FQ3 (June) itself was in line ex-tariff-refund: rev $109.4B (+16.4%) vs Street $108.85B, EPS $2.02 reported but ~$1.91 ex the +$0.11 refund vs Street $1.89, GM 50.1% reported but ~48.1% ex ~2pp refund vs Street 48.0-48.1%. The guide is what broke: SEPT-Q REVENUE +9-11% y/y vs Street +12.1%, and GM 47-48% including ~1pt of refund benefit = ~46.5% UNDERLYING, a large miss to buy-side bars. Management's own attribution is the durable fact: memory costs explained "MORE THAN 100% OF" the sequential GM decline (49.3% → ~48.1%), the Sept-Q ~46.5% is "also mostly explained by memory cost pressure," and beyond September the carry-in-inventory cushion fades while market pricing keeps rising — Cook called it a "hundred-year flood on the memory pricing with exponential increases" and said price rises were taken "reluctantly." Second, separate constraint: advanced-node SoC availability at TSMC, now limiting iPhone, Mac AND iPad, set to "increase significantly sequentially," with management REFUSING to give comfort that it moderates by the December quarter and giving nothing on the INTC SoC engagement. Segment detail: iPhone $54.25B and Mac $10.35B beat, but Services MISSED at $30.74B vs Street $31.58B (+12% vs +13%) with Services GM 75.6% also short, and Greater China $18.8B vs Street $19.5B. Capex only $2.4B in the quarter ($6.8B YTD vs $9.5B LY); DOI rose to 18 days from 11, read as memory stockpiling. Stock -4% on the release, -6% to -7.2% through the call. This was Tim Cook's last call as CEO. The read-across is the actionable part and it inverted the July trade: MU +~3% and SNDK +~5% after-hours on Apple's own commentary, while Apple's stated plan to offset memory with "lower costs on certain non-memory components" is a negative for the rest of the BOM (QCOM named explicitly by Jefferies). Bernstein's pre-print pair — long AMZN / avoid AAPL at ATHs on "memory/pricing headwinds" — worked on both legs the same evening. |
| 2026-07-29 | MSFT | bull won — the Q4 FY26 print (07-29 AMC) closed this catalyst; **full post-mortem is logged under `MSFT 2026-07-30`** below (the ledger entry was filed against the reaction date, leaving the 07-29 catalyst bullet flagged). Summary: the modelled negative-FCF year did not arrive (Q4 OCF $55.4B/+30%, **FCF +$19.6B**, FY27 guided FCF-positive, against UBS's -$21B and BofA's $32B trough), and Azure +43% (~42% cc) beat both the 39-40% cc guide and every bogey on the page, with Q1 FY27 guided ~+45% cc. Two qualifiers carried over: the reported $4.74 EPS includes $0.27 of discrete benefit (a $3.2B Anthropic mark-up), so clean EPS is ~$4.47; and CY26 capex moving ~$190B → ~$175B is a **finance-to-operating lease reclassification**, not a spending cut, which invalidates every FY27 capex bogey on the page as a like-for-like comparison. The FCF-sign debate is closed; the funding-gap and capital-structure debate stays open minus its near-term trigger, and the UBS Evidence Lab / Business Insider / Archera Azure-rationing evidence was left unmentioned and unrefuted. |
| 2026-07-29 | META | bear won — both catalysts dated to the Q2'26 call came due and **neither delivered its clearing content**, and the stock fell 8-9% specifically for that reason. (1) **Equity-raise decision — NOT resolved:** no raise announced, and Susan Li deferred the whole question to the 2027 planning cycle ("we don't have any plans to share… as we plan for our investments in 2027, I think we will certainly evaluate all of the financing options"), naming operating cash flow as "the primary pillar" plus IG debt and partnerships like the BlackRock 1GW El Paso JV. So the near-term dilution risk did not materialise, but neither did the clearing event BofA flagged (2026-06-11) — the overhang simply persists with no dated trigger. (2) **"Meta Compute" build-out and disclosure — only PARTIALLY resolved:** management confirmed the effort by name and Zuckerberg twice said Meta is "getting a lot of offers for Compute at a **significant premium over what we paid**," but gave **no scale, no pricing, no GW committed to third parties, no enterprise logo and no dollar of revenue**, and explicitly declined to rank which monetisation leg scales first. JPM's Anmuth: "we did **not** come away learning much incremental on the developer API, consumer/business agents, or potential plans to directly monetize compute." That vacuum is the post-mortem: JPM's desk titled it "**META Q2 'Trust Me, Bro'**" — investors were "surprised at the magnitude" of the drawdown, with "underwhelming" and "generic" the most-cited adjectives — while **2027 EPS estimates barely moved** (most still $33-36), i.e. a multiple/narrative event, not an estimate event. The numbers themselves were fine-to-better-than-feared (rev $60.80B +28% vs Street $60.2B; Q3 guide $61-64B "smack in line with buyside"; FY26 opex ceiling $169B and capex ceiling $145B both untouched; ex-$2.4B legal and $1.2B severance, operating income would have **risen 9% y/y** vs the reported -8%). Three things the call did add, all of which survive the bear verdict: management is on the record **demand-constrained** ("nowhere near enough Compute for all the demand"; "numerous ROI-positive places we would put Compute toward if we had it") — first-party support for MS/Joe Moore's "excess demand, not excess supply" and against the "Meta Compute = overcapacity" read that hit CRWV/NBIS on 07-01; the preference for **selling intelligence over raw compute** keeps merchant compute tighter for longer (Rothschild/Adley); and the ads stack delivered its first quantified in-model gains (generative recommender; GEM + sequence learning **+8.3% ad clicks / +15.7% conversion uplift on Facebook**; Advantage+ at a **>$75B run-rate**). ⚠ Both replacement catalysts are now dated to the **Q3'26 print (late Oct 2026)** — the customary first FY27 capex guide — where the page's spread is unresolved and now also a **basis** argument: $142B (Redburn, reported, after off-BS JVs) / $180-200B (Redburn, gross build) / $243B (JPM Anmuth). Track cash PP&E, not reported capex. |

| 2026-07-27 | SANM | neutral (mixed) — F3Q26 beat on the P&L and slipped on the ramp. Revenue at the high end of the $3.2-3.5B guide, OM 8.0% and EPS $3.31 both above the high end, with core Sanmina accelerating to +17% y/y (from +8% in F2Q26) on broader Industrial strength and comms/cloud infra +33% y/y. But ZT Systems only met its guide at $1.1B, the F4Q ZT guide slipped to $0.8-1.0B, and management expects ZERO AMD Helios rack revenue in F4Q26 because revenue is recognised only when customers physically receive racks in their datacenter — pushing Helios to F1Q27 and ZT overall to F2H27. Management reiterated $16B+ FY27, so this reads as phasing rather than lost demand. Margin-quality caveat: ~100bps of the operating-margin upside came from non-recurring engineering services that moderate as production ramps. The three covering houses split on the same print — JPM cut to $260 (from $275), BofA raised to $270 (from $234), Hosseini raised to $210 (from $185, essentially at spot) — all still Neutral. The bear reframes from 'are ZT margins real' to 'when does ZT actually land'. |
| 2026-07-27 | CDNS | bull won — Q2 FY26 beat-and-raise into an unusually bearish setup: rev $1.584bn in line, OpM 45.5% (vs 45.0% exp), non-GAAP EPS $2.11 (vs $2.05), record backlog $8.1bn (from $8.0bn), IP $238m/+40% y/y well above (GS $209m/St $216m), Core EDA +18%, another record hardware quarter (12 new customers); FY26 raised to $6.26-6.34bn ($6.30bn mid, +19% y/y) and EPS $8.05-8.15 from $7.85-7.95, Q3 guided ~5% above consensus. The bar it cleared is the story: JPM's buy-side survey went in at -1 positioning, "the worst EDA sentiment I've seen," light with a small short bias near multi-year P/E lows, respondents evenly split on whether the FY guide would rise at all. GS raised PT $410->$470 (45x on normalized EPS lifted $9.10->$10.50) yet still expects the stock range-bound; MS OW sits at $370. Debate moves off execution and onto whether agentic AI is accretive to EDA seat value or a disruption risk. |
| 2026-07-24 | SPCX | bull won — Starship Flight 13 operationally successful, clearing the overhang left by the July 17 launch abort. MS (Adam Jonas) grades it 'A-' and says it materially improved confidence in Starship's path toward operational service: clean ascent on all 33 Super Heavy engines, successful hot-stage separation, FIRST deployment of 20 production Starlink V3 satellites, successful in-space Raptor relight, and the best reentry/splashdown to date with the ship intact and transmitting telemetry (Bernstein notes the splashdown itself was hard). Next catalyst dated and larger: Musk indicated Flight 14 may attempt the first-ever CATCH of a Starship upper stage on the Mechazilla tower arms, expected late Aug / early Sep 2026, likely requiring Starship's first orbital trajectory; Bernstein puts full reuse in sight at Flight 15. Both houses note the market has yet to underwrite these launches, with the lockup overhang starting to dissipate after 2Q/3Q EPS (8/4). |
| 2026-07-24 | GOOG | neutral — "Frozen v2" was a monitoring/specification bullet, not a dated event; the parser took the date of the Jefferies note that specified it. What actually resolved on 07-24: **Jefferies · Blayne Curtis ("Blayne's Bytes")** put detail on The Information's 07-20 report — the internal nickname is **"Frozen v2"**, the mechanism is **hardwiring elements of Gemini directly into silicon** (fewer calculations, less data movement per query), and Google engineers project **6-10x more tokens per unit of power vs the latest TPUs**. But Curtis named the constraints that cap it: it is "**more experimental rather than a replacement for the TPU program**," **deployment targeted for C28 with volumes expected well below TPU levels**, and **flexibility limited to Gemini versions that keep the same core architecture** (a model-specific ASIC that obsoletes when the architecture changes); supply chain "**could potentially be a MRVL design (unconfirmed)**." Reads as a **cost-per-token option, not a TPU-roadmap event** — nothing changes on the 2026-27 capex line, which is what the stock is actually trading on (Q2 print 07-22: FY26 capex raised to $195-205B, FCF $(5.9)B, stock −3 to −4%, MS/Nowak PT $415→$400 with '27/'28 EPS −7%/−5% on 07-23). Already fully captured on the page; no thesis rewrite needed. Logged neutral. (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, 2026-07-24; The Information, 2026-07-20) |
| 2026-07-24 | AMD | bull won — "Advancing AI 2026" (Jul 22-23) + the 07-24 recap layer scored the pre-event debate list, and the event over-delivered on content. Landed: **Anthropic as the 4th GW-scale MI customer** (up to 2GW MI450/Helios from 1H27, **$5B AMD equity, no warrants**; UBS/BofA read it as +$10-15bn to the CY27 MI consensus of $35-40bn → DC could double to $60-65bn+ in 2027); the **Microsoft Helios** commitment formalized 07-20 (MI455X + Venice + Pensando + ROCm, ships 2H26); **all three CY30 TAMs raised at once — total compute $1Tn→$2Tn, AI accelerators →$1.4Tn, server CPU $120bn→$220bn** — above BofA's pre-called $1.5tn/$200bn (GS/Schneider 07-23; BofA/Arya 07-24); the **Cerebras disaggregated-inference tie-up** (~5x throughput, Cerebras Cloud by end-2026) resolving BofA's pre-event debate #2 via partnership, which MS frames as the answer to NVDA/Groq; **ROCm.ai**; and Meta on stage on a 4th consecutive EPYC generation. The single most important independent mark: **SemiAnalysis — the harshest ROCm critic on the page — upgraded from "0% chance" → "a great chance of success"** (2026-07-24), conditional on two named gates. PT ladder moved up (UBS/Arcuri $730 on 07-25, the high; Barclays $665; BofA $620 reiterated; GS Buy $640), no MI-revenue guide-up given, as expected. **Why not a clean bull:** the stock reaction was muted into a risk-off tape (Barclays "somewhat surprised… a customer expansion wasn't expected at the AD"); **MS stays EW $410** on warrants-as-marketing-expense ("wipes out most of the profits from GPU for the next few years") — now quantified from the customer side by SemiAnalysis at a **~$16.0B/GW effective rebate vs ~$15.2B/GW paid by OpenAI and ~$18.7B/GW by Meta (~105%/~85% discounts)**; and **2 of BofA's 5 pre-event debates are still open** — (1) how Anthropic splits share across TPU/Trainium/NVDA/AMD (now the single most valuable unanswered question on the page, see ANTHROPIC) and (5) sufficient leading-edge TSMC capacity across MI450/EPYC/Client. Three dated software milestones were **missed** at the event (90%-CUDA vLLM gating; Pollara/Kubernetes CI at 0% parity with NVDA ConnectX nightly; MI455X gfx1250 nightly CI slipped to Oct-2026). Next hard checkpoint: the **Q2 FY26 print, reported by GS/BofA as Aug-4** (guide $11.2B), where the forward test is the structure of the next GW-scale deal (equity vs warrants). |
